Top 5,000 Market Instruments

A category-aware public market-activity directory across global shares, funds and ETFs, bonds, futures and commodities, crypto, forex, and indices.

As of 2026-11-04. This is not a universal “most searched” ranking. No public source reports comparable worldwide search counts for 5,000 instruments. The list uses a transparent category-balanced order; each row carries its own rank basis, source, country, and date.

Open the full Markets Today directory · Download the Top 50,000 directory data

Methodology and source notes

The 5,000 entries are the first 5,000 rows from a balanced 50,000-item category index. Each asset class is ranked separately; positions across unlike categories are not a single comparable popularity score. Market capitalization and reported trading volume are market-visibility proxies, not direct web searches.

  • Stocks & shares: Market capitalization, descending — TradingView Global Scanner, checked 2026-11-04.
  • Funds, ETFs & REITs: Reported trading volume, descending — TradingView Global Scanner, checked 2026-11-04.
  • Bonds: Reported trading volume, descending — TradingView Global Scanner, checked 2026-11-04.
  • Futures & commodities: Reported contract volume, descending — TradingView Global Scanner, checked 2026-11-04.
  • Cryptocurrency markets: Reported exchange trading volume, descending; CoinGecko trend signal is annotated where matched — TradingView Global Scanner, checked 2026-11-04.
  • Forex & currency pairs: Reported currency-pair volume, descending — TradingView Global Scanner, checked 2026-11-04.
  • World indices: Reported market activity where available; otherwise provider directory order — TradingView Global Scanner, checked 2026-11-04.

For an additional platform-specific search signal, CoinGecko's public Trending Search reports its trending coins for the last 24 hours (default top 15). Yahoo Finance's Trending Tickers and a public report on ETF.com comparison-tool interest are separate platform signals and do not publish an all-market, 50,000-row query-count ranking. Google Trends uses normalized relative interest, not absolute counts.
